## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T15:06:27.197Z

Since the worktree dialog has no content editing and content ties to message.content, I realize for forking as assistant the prompt needs to come from the user instead—so for worktree creation, I should use window.prompt to collect the content first, then open the dialog with that value. Now I'm moving to the backend work in recovery.ts, starting with how recoverRun already handles preemption.

I need to extend the assistant-target case: when there's no user message row and the action is fork, fall through to a new forkFromAssistantMessage path instead of throwing. I'm considering refactoring the existing fork logic into a shared helper that takes the run, recovery args, messages to copy, checkpoint data, and attachments, but I'm weighing whether to keep the existing user-fork block mostly intact and just parametrize the pieces that differ, since the current loop already copies messages up to the target and replaces the tail.

For timestamps, I'm deciding whether copied messages should keep their original createdAt values or all get reset to "now" like the existing code does -- preserving originals should keep ordering correct since the new checkpoint message would still land after them chronologically, but I'm wary this could break some "messages since run start" logic that gates on timestamps relative to the run's creation time, so leaning toward keeping the safer "now" approach for now.

I'm also questioning whether the copied user messages should even show up in the forked conversation UI, since they'd appear without assistant replies, but this matches the existing fork behavior so I'll keep it consistent. For replay support, I need a new function to build a conversation transcript prefix through a specific entry, then append the next user prompt.

I'm now generalizing startDirectRerun's options to support forking from another run: adding a `forkReplay` option with sourceRunId and throughEntryId, alongside the existing replayTargetMessageId logic, so the replay builder can branch based on which mode is active.

I'm wondering whether the user-message fork path should also get replay-before-target treatment, since that would fix an existing context-loss bug for session forks. But making it throw where it previously succeeded feels risky if the target message isn't in the stream, so I'll leave that path unchanged and just flag the issue rather than fix it now — though I should double check whether it's actually losing context in practice.

I'm confirming that startDirectRerun spawns a fresh agent without session resume, using only the next content as the prompt, which is worth noting explicitly. I also need to make sure cancelRunWorkers isn't called on the source run during an assistant-type fork, since forking should leave the source untouched. On the frontend, shouldSelectRecoveredRunAfterSuccess already correctly selects the new run after a fork completes.

For validation, the target entry needs to be a "message" type within the visible transcript, and I should account for the "fork to different CLI" handoff from the assistant path. Now I'm restructuring the beginning of recoverRunUnlocked to check for the target message and handle the fork action case.

Writing fork helper function...

Still writing fork logic...

I'm working out the error messages so they match the expected route validation strings—distinguishing "prompt required" from "target must be a user or assistant message" cases, and settling on "not found" phrasing with a 404 for missing targets. I also need to double check that the timestamp used for coalesced assistant entries reflects the first chunk, so the history filter correctly captures only messages created before that point.

I'm also realizing that forking from an assistant message shouldn't touch the source run, but running it inside runConversationMutation could deadlock: a queued delivery holds that mutation while its provider turn runs, so forking mid-turn would block until the turn completes. I need to route the assistant fork case outside that mutation to avoid this.

Not my concern here — I'll just keep parity with existing behavior. Now I need to think about how handoff candidates change, plus the frontend side, but I'll write the backend first.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T15:08:52.837Z

I'm noticing handleForkMessageToDifferentCli is defined inline in HomeApp rather than memoized with useCallback, so its identity changes every render, which cascades into getAssistantMessageActions changing and breaking the ActivityRow memo — causing every row to re-render unnecessarily. This same issue seems to have existed before for getUserMessageActions too.

The real fix is to store the latest handlers in a ref inside ConversationMain so the callback passed down stays stable across renders, rather than relying on the parent components to memoize properly. I'm checking whether the codebase already has a useLatest or useEvent-style helper for this pattern, since assigning to a ref during render might trigger lint warnings.

parent: febd084a-e4f4-4781-8189-a497b682fef0

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T15:09:00.935Z

I'm checking whether handleRetryMessage and handleStartEditingMessage are actually stable references, since HomeApp passes them from useConversationActions which returns new plain functions each render — meaning they aren't stable, so the "must be stable" comment on getUserMessageActions is aspirational rather than enforced. I also want to verify whether ConversationMain is wrapped in memo.

Since ActivityRow likely already receives unstable handlers like handleCancelEditingMessage and handleProjectFileReferenceClick from HomeApp, its memoization is probably already broken on HomeApp renders regardless of my change. Still, I'll be a good citizen and make getAssistantMessageActions stable by storing the fork handlers in a ref updated via useEffect, since these actions are only invoked on click so stale refs are safe.

lint exit 0
  it("forks a new direct conversation from a direct user checkpoint", async () => {
    mockAskAgent.mockClear();
    mockCancelAgent.mockClear();
    mockGetAgent.mockClear();
    mockSpawnAgent.mockClear();
    mockStartSupervisorRun.mockClear();
    const planId = randomUUID();
    const runId = randomUUID();
    const userMessageId = randomUUID();
    const adHocRelativePath = path.join("vibes", "ad-hoc", `${randomUUID()}.md`);
    const adHocAbsolutePath = getAppDataPath(adHocRelativePath);

    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(adHocAbsolutePath), { recursive: true });
    fs.writeFileSync(adHocAbsolutePath, "# temp\nsource prompt");

    await db.insert(plans).values({
      id: planId,
      path: adHocRelativePath,
      status: "running",
      createdAt: new Date(),
      updatedAt: new Date(),
    });

    await db.insert(runs).values({
      id: runId,
      planId,
      mode: "direct",
      title: "Source run",
      projectPath: "/workspace/app",
      preferredWorkerType: "codex",
      preferredWorkerModel: "gpt-5.4",
      preferredWorkerEffort: "medium",
      allowedWorkerTypes: JSON.stringify(["codex", "opencode"]),
      status: "done",
      createdAt: new Date(),
      updatedAt: new Date(),
    });

    await db.insert(messages).values({
      id: userMessageId,
      runId,
      role: "user",
      kind: "checkpoint",
      content: "source prompt",
      createdAt: new Date("2026-04-21T10:00:00Z"),
    });

    const request = new Request(`http://localhost/api/runs/${runId}`, {
      method: "POST",
      body: JSON.stringify({ action: "fork", targetMessageId: userMessageId, content: "forked prompt" }),
    });

    const response = await POST(request, { params: Promise.resolve({ id: runId }) });
    expect(response.status).toBe(200);

    const payload = await response.json();
    const forkedRun = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, payload.runId)).get();
    const forkedMessages = await db.select().from(messages).where(eq(messages.runId, payload.runId));
    const forkedPlan = await db.select().from(plans).where(eq(plans.id, forkedRun!.planId)).get();

    expect(payload.runId).not.toBe(runId);
    expect(payload.runId).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{12}$/);
    expect(forkedRun?.parentRunId).toBe(runId);
    expect(forkedRun?.forkedFromMessageId).toBe(userMessageId);
    expect(forkedRun?.mode).toBe("direct");
    expect(forkedRun?.projectPath).toBe("/workspace/app");
    expect(forkedRun?.preferredWorkerType).toBe("codex");
    expect(forkedRun?.preferredWorkerModel).toBe("gpt-5.4");
    expect(forkedRun?.preferredWorkerEffort).toBe("medium");
    expect(forkedRun?.allowedWorkerTypes).toBe(JSON.stringify(["codex", "opencode"]));
    // Worker response now lives in the unified worker stream.
    expect(forkedMessages.map((message) => message.role)).toEqual(["user"]);
    expect(forkedMessages[0]?.content).toBe("forked prompt");
    expect(fs.readFileSync(getAppDataPath(forkedPlan!.path), "utf-8")).toContain("forked prompt");
    expect(mockStartSupervisorRun).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
    expect(mockSpawnAgent).toHaveBeenCalledWith(expect.objectContaining({
      type: "codex",
      cwd: "/workspace/app",
      model: "gpt-5.4",
      effort: "medium",
    }));
    expect(mockAskAgent).toHaveBeenCalledWith(
      expect.any(String),
      "forked prompt",
      undefined,
      { expectedTurnGeneration: 0 },
    );
  });

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T15:10:11.104Z

I'm planning a test after the existing fork test, seeding a running conversation with a worker containing two user/assistant exchanges, then forking at the first assistant message with new content "follow-up" to verify the fork behaves correctly.

Forked messages should have roles [user, user] and contents ["prompt A", "follow-up"] in creation order, with the ask prompt including "prompt A" and "answer A" but excluding "answer B" and "prompt B", ending in "follow-up". I need to confirm the source worker stays uncancelled and that forkedFromMessageId equals "assist-a", then match the worker insert field shape used earlier in the file (id, runId, type, status, cwd, outputLog, currentText, lastText, createdAt, updatedAt) to write this new worker row.

## Claude · 2026-09-28T15:13:07.793Z

I moved Fork from the user message to the assistant reply. It sits in the hover bar next to Copy, with the same three options as before: Fork from here, Fork into worktree, and Fork to other CLI. User messages keep Copy, Retry and Edit. Typecheck and lint are clean, and I haven't committed anything.

**What forking a reply does now:**
- It asks for your next message ("Continue the fork with:", starting empty).
- It opens a new conversation. The agent there gets the visible transcript up to and including that reply, then your new message.
- The original conversation is left alone. Its workers aren't cancelled, and you can fork it even while it's in the middle of a turn.
- "Fork into worktree" asks for the message first, then opens the worktree dialog.
- "Fork to other CLI" now accepts a reply as the cut-off point, so the handoff includes that reply and everything before it.

The main changes are in `src/server/runs/recovery.ts` (the old and new fork paths now share one helper), `src/server/handoff/candidates.ts`, `src/components/Terminal.tsx` and `src/components/home/ConversationMain.tsx`.

**Tests:** I added two API tests for the new fork, a new test file for the handoff cut-off point, and updated one UI test. In the full suite, 3464 tests passed and 2 failed:
- `mac-install-workflow` fails because `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yml` doesn't exist.
- `process-session-restart` failed during the full run but passes when run alone, so it looks flaky.

Neither test touches the code I changed.

**Two things to know:**
- **The forked conversation only shows your earlier messages.** The agent has the full context, but the new conversation's view doesn't show the earlier assistant replies. The old fork behaved the same way. Showing them would mean copying the transcript into the new conversation, which is a bigger change.
- **"Fork session" in the header still works the old way.** It re-runs your latest message in the new conversation with no earlier context. Moving it onto the latest reply would fix that, but I kept it out of scope. Say if you want it.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T19:10:02.721Z

I need the visible transcript entries copied into the new run so the UI renders them properly. I'm considering seeding those entries into the new worker's stream before the user input is appended, possibly via a `seedEntries` option in startDirectRerun, though I need to check how the bridge's snapshot persistence might interact with that.

Alternatively, I could create a separate, already-completed "history" worker in the new run holding the copied entries, since the run-wide transcript merges across workers by timestamp anyway. But I need to be careful this history worker doesn't get mistakenly picked as the target for follow-up messages, and that it doesn't clutter the workers sidebar or mess with counts like conversationRanOnMultipleWorkers.

Seeding directly into the new worker's stream keeps things simpler with a single worker, though I need to check how persistWorkerSnapshot appends bridge entries and whether ids/timestamps could collide or reorder badly — copied entries keep their source ids and older timestamps, but since ordering is by seq within a single worker that should be fine.

I also need to apply this same "show full previous conversation" requirement to the user-checkpoint fork path, not just the bridge fork — seeding entries before the target user message would also fix the context-loss issue there. But if the target user_input isn't found in the stream, I'll need a fallback where seeding/replay is just skipped rather than throwing, making it best-effort.

For the general design, forkDirectRun should take an inheritedEntries list built by the caller, and startDirectRerun should take seedEntries and build its replay prompt directly from those using the existing renderTranscriptReplayPrompt export — replacing the current forkReplay option with something cleaner.

The trickier part is the messages table copy: since workerEntryMatchesUserMessage likely matches by id, copied messages with fresh random ids would cause duplicate rendering against the seeded user_input entries. I need to keep ids aligned by generating new ids for copied messages and rewriting the seeded user_input entries to use those same new ids via a sourceMessageId → newId map, while leaving other entry ids untouched since those aren't reused across runs. Now I want to check workerEntryMatchesUserMessage's matching logic and look at persistWorkerSnapshot to confirm seeded entries won't get clobbered when the bridge output is appended to the stream.

export type AppendWorkerEntryResult = {
  entry: WorkerEntry;
  appended: boolean;
  rejectedReason?: "turn_generation_changed";
};

export async function appendWorkerEntryWithResult(
  runId: string,
  workerId: string,
  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">,
  options: { expectedTurnGeneration?: number } = {},
): Promise<AppendWorkerEntryResult> {
  return runOnChain(runId, workerId, async () => {
    return withWorkerFileLock(runId, workerId, async (paths) => {
      const generation = await readWorkerTurnGenerationMatch(workerId, options.expectedTurnGeneration);
      if (!generation.matches) {
        await recordStaleWorkerOutputIgnored({
          runId,
          workerId,
          expectedTurnGeneration: options.expectedTurnGeneration!,
          currentTurnGeneration: generation.currentTurnGeneration,
          source: "entry_append",
        });
        return {
          entry: { ...(entry as WorkerEntry), seq: 0 },
          appended: false,
          rejectedReason: "turn_generation_changed" as const,
        };
      }
      // If a compaction race somehow stranded the plaintext file under .gz,
      // expand it first so cache refresh sees the live transcript rather
      // than the lock-created empty placeholder.
      await expandWorkerOutputFileInternal(paths);
      // Never resume numbering above a head that isn't on disk.
      await healStrandedStreamHead(runId, workerId, paths);

      const { nextSeq, seen, fingerprints } = await refreshChainCaches(runId, workerId);
      if (entry.id && seen.has(entry.id)) {
        // Already persisted; load it back so the caller has a stable
        // record. Linear scan is fine: this is the dedup path and runs at
        // append cadence, not read cadence.
        const persisted = await readCanonicalPersistedEntries(runId, workerId);
        const match = persisted.find((line) => line.id === entry.id);
        if (match && typeof match.seq === "number") {
          return { entry: match, appended: false };
        }
        // Legacy line without seq: assign one virtually based on file
        // position. The writer doesn't advance nextSeq in this case.
        return { entry: { ...(entry as WorkerEntry), seq: 0 }, appended: false };
      }

      const persistedEntry: WorkerEntry = {
        ...(entry as WorkerEntry),
        seq: nextSeq,
      };
      const compact = compactEntryForHistory(persistedEntry as unknown as CompactableEntry) as unknown as WorkerEntry;
      const line = JSON.stringify(compact) + "\n";

      const target = paths.filePath;
      const newlineGuard = await needsLeadingNewline(target);
      // Capture pre-append size so we can record the byte offset of the
      // new line in the sparse seq→offset index. The newline-guard byte
      // (if any) is written before the JSON line, so the line starts one
      // byte past sizeBefore in that case.
      let sizeBefore = 0;
      try {
        sizeBefore = (await fs.stat(target)).size;
      } catch (error) {
        if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ENOENT") throw error;
      }
      const lineStartOffset = sizeBefore + (newlineGuard ? 1 : 0);
      const handle = await fs.open(target, "a");
      try {
        await handle.appendFile(newlineGuard ? "\n" + line : line, "utf8");
        await handle.sync();
      } finally {
        await handle.close();
      }

      if (shouldIndex(persistedEntry.seq)) {
        // Best-effort: appendIndexEntry swallows its own errors. The
        // index is opportunistic — a failure here only slows future tail
        // reads until the next rebuild.
        await appendIndexEntry(target, {
          seq: persistedEntry.seq,
          offset: lineStartOffset,
        });
      }

      if (entry.id) {
        seen.add(entry.id);
        fingerprints.set(entry.id, workerEntryFingerprint(compact));
      }
      nextSeqByKey.set(chainKey(runId, workerId), nextSeq + 1);
      await rememberWorkerFileState(runId, workerId);
      const { commitArtifactAppend } = await import("@/server/artifacts/stream-metadata");
      await commitArtifactAppend({
        streamId: { runId, kind: "worker_entries", ownerId: workerId },
        seq: compact.seq,
        recordId: compact.id ?? `seq-${compact.seq}`,
      });
      return { entry: compact, appended: true };
    });
  });
}

export async function appendWorkerEntry(
  runId: string,
  workerId: string,
  entry: Omit<WorkerEntry, "seq">,
): Promise<WorkerEntry> {
  return (await appendWorkerEntryWithResult(runId, workerId, entry)).entry;
}

/**

export async function writeWorkerOutputEntries(
  runId: string,

      const generation = await readWorkerTurnGenerationMatch(workerId, options.expectedTurnGeneration);

        // It is a render-time hint, not real conversation content — never
        // persist it into the unified worker stream.
        if (entry.id === "output-archive-marker") {

        }

        // hands us synthetic snapshots may not. In that case, treat the entry

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T19:10:44.595Z

I'm sorting through which seed types should be included versus excluded—keeping user_input and supervisor_input since those seem safely actionable, while being cautious about config_option, permission/elicitation requests, and plan widgets since those could resurface unwanted state.

Since tool calls and updates share a toolCallId and readVisibleConversationTranscript coalesces entries by id, I should end up with one clean entry per id already, so diagnosticOnly entries are naturally filtered out of the visible transcript. I'm also realizing I can reuse the existing buildConversationTranscriptReplayPromptBeforeEntry mechanism to construct the replay prompt from the seeded entries before the new user input, since that edit path already handles prefixing correctly.

For startDirectRerun, seeding needs to happen right after the worker row is inserted but before appendUserInputOnDelivery, using a new seedEntries option appended with expectedTurnGeneration. I'll pass replayTargetMessageId only when seedEntries is nonempty, and adjust the event summary wording based on whether seeding occurred rather than hardcoding it. Since supersedeDiscardedBranch excludes the current workerId and there are no other workers to worry about, and seeding should occur before spawnAgent so the UI shows history immediately, this ordering should work cleanly with turn generation starting at 0.

Now I need to figure out id remapping: copied message rows get new ids via a sourceId→newId map, seeded user_input entries with mapped ids get remapped while unmapped ones and supervisor inputs keep their original ids, and attachments just reference the same storage paths so no changes needed there. I also want to restructure the code so forkDirectRun takes an inheritedEntries array instead of forkReplay, removing the forkReplay option and the replay-through-entry helper I'd added earlier, and add a small helper to filter and strip seq/workerId from entries before inheriting them. Now I'm going to look at the current forkDirectRun and startDirectRerun implementations to make these edits.

<!-- codex-event:{"kind":"reasoning","timestamp":"2026-09-28T19:12:50.598Z","phase":null} -->
## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T19:12:50.598Z

Since failures get persisted before rethrow, backgrounding startDirectRerun should be safe, but I need to make sure seeding finishes before returning so the UI has history ready immediately rather than relying on the event stream to catch up.

I'm thinking the cleanest approach is to have forkDirectRun run startDirectRerun in the background but wait on a "seeded" signal before returning, using a callback that resolves once seeding completes, raced against the rerun promise itself so any early failure still propagates correctly.

Since trackConversationBackgroundTask already attaches handlers to the tracked task, rejections after the race settles are still handled safely. I'm leaning toward resolving the seeded signal right after the seeding loop finishes rather than waiting for the slower user-input append, since the message row already exists and can render via fallback.

For tests that check mockAskAgent synchronously after the POST response, I'll need to use waitForConversationBackgroundTasksForTests since the ask may now happen in the background. I should also verify the route still calls notifyEventStreamSubscribers after recoverRun before moving to implementation.



uuid: 7a41427b-6b75-4cff-8e0f-a444b9fc7556
parent: 5cd6cbdf-ac64-42d2-bdde-a6eca497ddfa

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-28T19:12:50.598Z

Since failures inside `startDirectRerun` are already persisted before it throws, it's safe to run it in the background — the fork call returns as soon as history is seeded, so you land in the fork right away.

## Claude · 2026-09-28T19:16:25.634Z

A fork now shows the whole previous conversation. Your earlier messages, the assistant's replies, thoughts and tool calls all appear in the new conversation, and it continues after them with your new message.

**How it works:**
- The server copies the history into the new conversation before its first turn: everything up to and including the reply you forked from.
- Your copied messages keep the same ids as their history entries, so nothing shows up twice.
- The agent's context is built from that same copied history, so what you see is exactly what it knows.
- Some things aren't copied because they belong to the original's live session: pending permission and question prompts, plans, and session bookkeeping.
- **Fork session** in the header gets the same treatment. Its fork used to start with no earlier context at all; now it carries the history too.

I also fixed a lag I found on the way. The fork request used to wait for the new conversation's whole first turn, so you stayed on the original until the agent finished. Now it returns once the history is copied and the first turn runs in the background, so you land in the fork straight away with the history visible. If that first turn fails, the error shows up on the forked conversation.

The assistant-fork test now checks that the new conversation contains the first prompt, the first reply and your new message, in that order. Three existing fork tests needed a wait for the background turn. Typecheck and lint are clean. In the full suite the only failures are the same two from before, neither touching fork code: `mac-install-workflow` (missing workflow file) and `process-session-restart` (passes when run alone). Nothing is committed.

One gap: if the original conversation is in the middle of a turn, a tool call that started before the forked reply but finished after it will show as still running in the fork.
